But no longer will they be vulnerable to our earlier methods of attack. Your spies report that the first plane, the plane which was first attacked by the *Solarite*, is still undergoing repairs. These will be completed within two days, and then, when they can leave a base guard of two ships, they will attack once more. Furthermore, they will attack with a new weapon. They have destroyed the usefulness of our weapon, invisibility, and in turn, now have it to use against us! We must seek out some new weapon. I hope we are on the right track now, but every moment is precious, and we must get back to the work. This address must be short. Later, when we have completed our preliminary work, we will have to give plans to your workmen, which you will be able to turn into metal, for we lack the materials. With this help we may succeed, despite our handicap.”

The address was terminated at once. The Lanorians were probably disappointed, but they fully realized the necessity for haste.

“I wish Terrestrian orators spoke like that,” remarked Morey as they returned to the ship. “He said all there was to say, but he didn’t run miles of speech doing it. He was a very forceful speaker, too!”

“People who speak briefly and to the point generally are,” Arcot said.

===Chinese===
阿科特代表地球人答话。

「我谨代表我自己和在此的几位朋友——其中两位还没来得及学贵邦的语言——感谢你们在我们最需要时伸出的援手。诸位所救的，也许不止一座城——你们也许成全了一颗行星的得救——我们的地球。可这里的战斗才刚刚开场。

「卡克索里亚营中现有巨舰十八。它们同*索拉里特号*迄今三次交手，败得很惨。可它们再不会任由咱们早先的打法摆布了。你们的探子报说，头一架飞机——头一架遭*索拉里特号*攻击的那架——仍在修理，两天内完工；到那时，留两艘守营，余者将再度来攻。而且，他们将带着新武器来。咱们隐身这件武器的效用，已被他们破了，反倒落到他们手里，要冲咱们使了！咱们必须另觅新器。我希望眼下的路子是对的，可一刻千金，我们得回去赶工了。这番话只能短。稍后，初步的活计一完，我们还得把图纸交给你们的工匠——这些要靠你们化成金属，我们缺材料。有这一臂之力，尽管家底吃亏，我们或许能成。」

致辞就此打住。拉诺人多半意犹未尽，可他们完全明白事不宜迟。

「地球的演说家要都这么讲就好了，」回船路上莫雷叹道，「该说的全说了，却没扯出几里地的演词。而且他讲得多有力！」

「言简意赅的人，向来有力。」阿科特说。

===Original===
It was nearly noon that day before the theoretical discussion had been reduced to practical terms. They were ready to start work at once, but they had reason to work cheerfully now. Even through air they had found their ray would be able to reach thirty-five miles! They would be well out of the danger zone while attacking the gigantic planes of Kaxor.

Morey, Wade and Arcot at once set to work constructing the electrical plant that was to give them the necessary power. It was lucky indeed that they had brought the great mass of spare apparatus! They had more than enough to make all the electrical machinery. The tubes, the coils, the condensers, all were there. The generator would easily supply the power, for the terrific forces that were to destroy the Kaxorian ships were to be generated in the plane itself. It was to destroy itself; the *Solarite* would merely be the detonator to set it off!

---

While the physicists were busy on this, Fuller was designing the mechanical details of the projector. It must be able to turn through a spherical angle of 180 degrees, and was necessarily controlled electrically from the inside. The details of the projector were worked out by six that evening, and the numerous castings and machined pieces that were to be used were to be made in the Venerian machine shops.

One difficulty after another arose and was overcome. Night came on, and still they continued work. The Venerian workmen had promised to have the apparatus for them by ten o’clock the next morning—or what corresponded to ten o’clock.

===Chinese===
那天近午，理论的探讨才落成实用的条目。他们即刻可以开工，而且这一回干得有盼头。他们发现，连在空气里，这道射线也能打三十五英里！攻击卡克索巨机时，他们尽可以待在危险圈外了。

莫雷、韦德和阿科特立刻动手造发电设备，给它们出所需的功率。幸亏带了那一大堆备件！电气机器要多少有多少。管子、线圈、电容器，一应俱全。发电机供功率绰绰有余——因为要毁灭卡克索里亚巨舰的那些骇人之力，将在巨机自己体内生成。是它毁掉它自己；*索拉里特号*只当那点火的雷管！

---

物理学家们忙这个的当口，富勒在画投射器的机械细节。它必须能转过 180 度的球面角，又必须从舱内电控。到傍晚六点，投射器的细节全部敲定，要用的众多铸件和机加工件，交金星人的机工厂去做。

难题一个接一个冒头，又一个接一个被啃掉。夜来了，他们还在干。金星工匠答应次日上午十点——或者说他们的十点——把设备交货。

===Original===
Shortly after three o’clock that morning they had finished the apparatus, had connected all the controls, and had placed the last of the projector directors. Except for the projector they were ready, and Morey, Wade and Fuller turned in to get what sleep they could. But Arcot, telling them there was something he wished to get, took another dose of Immorpho and stepped out into the steaming rain.

A few minutes after ten the next morning Arcot came back, followed by half a dozen Venerians, each carrying a large metal cylinder in a cradle. These were attached to the landing gear of the *Solarite* in such fashion that the fusing of one piece of wire would permit the entire thing to drop free.

“So *that’s* what you hatched out, eh? What is it?” asked Wade as he entered the ship.

“Just a thing I want to try out—and I’m going to keep it a deep, dark secret for a while. I think you’ll get quite a surprise when you see those bombs in action! They’re arranged to be released by turning current into the landing lights. We’ll have to forgo lights for the present, but I needed the bombs more.

“The mechanics have finished working on your projector parts, Fuller, and they’ll be over here in a short time. Here comes the little gang I asked to help us. You can direct them.” Arcot paused and scowled with annoyance. “Hang it all—when they drill into the outer wall, we’ll lose the vacuum between the two walls, and all that hot air will come in. This place will be roasting in a short time. We have the molecular motion coolers, but I’m afraid they won’t be much good. Can’t use the generator—it’s cut off from the main room by vacuum wall.

“I think we’d better charge up the gas tanks and the batteries as soon as this is done. Then tonight we’ll attack the Kaxorian construction camp. I’ve just learned that no spy reports have been coming in, and I’m afraid they’ll spring a surprise.”

===Chinese===
凌晨三点过，设备完工，控制器全部接好，最后一具投射定向器装上了位。只差投射器本体，莫雷、韦德和富勒去抓紧眯一会儿。阿科特却说要取一样东西，又服了一剂不眠药，跨进热气腾腾的雨里。

次日上午十点过几分，阿科特回来了，身后跟着六个金星人，各用摇篮抬着一具大金属筒。它们被装上*索拉里特号*的起落架，装法讲究：熔断一根铁丝，整件便能脱落。

「原来你孵的是这个，嗯？是什么？」韦德在他进船时问。

「一件我想试试的东西——暂时还是个深藏的黑秘密。等见了这些炸弹开工，你们准会大吃一惊！它们用着陆灯的电流触发释放。灯暂时点不成灯了，可我宁要炸弹。

「富勒，你的投射器零件机械师们完工了，一会儿就送过来。我请来帮忙的小队也到了，你来调度。」阿科特顿了顿，懊恼地皱眉。「见鬼——他们往外壳上打孔，两层壁间的真空就完了，热气全灌进来。这地方转眼就是烤炉。分子运动冷却器倒是有，怕是不顶大用。发电机又使不上——真空墙把它隔在主舱外头。

「这活一完，我看最好把气罐和电池都充满。今晚咱们就攻击卡克索里亚建造营。我刚听说探子的报告断了些日子，怕是他们要搞突然袭击。」

===Original===
Somewhat later came the sound of drills, then the whistling roar as the air sucked into the vacuum, told the men inside that the work was under way. It soon became uncomfortably hot as, the vacuum destroyed, the heat came in through all sides. It was more than the little molecular coolers could handle, and the temperature soon rose to about a hundred and fifteen. It was not as bad as the Venerian atmosphere, for the air seemed exceedingly dry, and the men found it possible to get along without cooling suits, if they did not work. Since there was little they could do, they simply relaxed.

It was nearly dark before the Lanorians had finished their work, and the gas tanks had been recharged. All that time Arcot had spent with Tonlos determining the position of the Kaxorian construction camp. Spy reports and old maps had helped, but it was impossible to do very accurate work by these means.

It was finally decided that the Kaxorian construction camp was about 10,500 miles to the southwest. The *Solarite* was to start an hour after dark. Travelling westward at their speed, they hoped to reach the camp just after nightfall.

===Chinese===
稍晚，钻孔声响起，随即空气灌进真空、发出一声呼啸——舱里的人知道开工了。真空一毁，热气四面涌入，屋里很快热得难受。小小的分子冷却器招架不住，温度很快爬到一百一十五度上下。好在比金星大气还强些——空气干得很——不干活的话，不穿降温服也熬得住。反正帮不上忙，他们索性歇着。

拉诺人完工、气罐充好，天已擦黑。这一整段时间，阿科特都陪着通洛斯，测定卡克索里亚建造营的位置。探子的报告和旧地图帮了忙，可单凭这些，做不出太细的活。

末了议定：卡克索里亚建造营在西南约 10,500 英里处。*索拉里特号*天黑一小时后启程。以他们的速度向西飞，指望刚入夜便抵达营地上空。

## VIII / 八

===Original===
The *Solarite* sped swiftly toward the southwest. The sky slowly grew lighter as the miles flashed beneath them. They were catching up with the sun. As they saw the rolling ocean beneath them give way to low plains, they realized they were over Kaxorian land. The *Solarite* was flying very high, and as they showed no lights, and were not using the invisibility apparatus, they were practically undetectable. Suddenly they saw the lights of a mighty city looming far off to the east.

“It’s Kanor. Pass well to the west of it. That’s their capital. We’re on course.” Arcot spoke from his position at the projector, telling Wade the directions to follow on his course to the berth of the giant planes.

The city dropped far behind them in moments, followed by another, and another. At length, veering southward into the dusk, they entered a region of low hills, age-old folds in the crust of the planet, rounded by untold millennia of torrential rains.

“Easy, Wade. We are near now.” Mile after mile they flashed ahead at about a thousand miles an hour—then suddenly they saw far off to the east a vast glow that reached into the sky, painting itself on the eternal clouds miles above.

“There it is, Wade. Go high, and take it easy!”

===Chinese===
*索拉里特号*朝西南疾驰。英里在脚下飞掠，天色渐渐亮起来——他们正追着太阳跑。见脚下起伏的海洋让位给低平的平原，他们知道已到了卡克索里亚的领空。*索拉里特号*飞得极高，又不点灯、不开隐身机，几乎无从察觉。蓦地，他们望见东边远处一座大城的灯火。

「那是卡诺。从它西边远远绕过去。是他们的都城。航线没错。」阿科特守在投射器旁，指挥韦德按航线飞向巨机的泊地。

那座城片刻便落到身后，随后又一座，又一座。末了，他们转向南，扎进暮色，进入一片低丘地带——行星地壳里古老的褶皱，被亿万年滂沱大雨磨圆了。

「稳住，韦德。快到了。」他们以约每小时一千英里的速度一英里接一英里地掠过——突然，东边远处现出一片泼天的辉光，映在高空几英里外的永世云层上。

「就是那儿，韦德。升高，稳着来！」

===Original===
Swiftly the *Solarite* climbed, hovering at last on the very rim of the cloud blanket, an invisible mote in a sea of gray mist. Below them they saw a tremendous field carved, it seemed, out of the ancient hills. From this height all sense of proportion was lost. It seemed but an ordinary field, with eighteen ordinary airplanes resting on it. One of these now was moving, and in a moment it rose into the air! But there seemed to be no men on all the great field. They were invisibly small from this height.

Abruptly Arcot gave a great shout. “That’s their surprise! They’re ready far ahead of the time we expected! If all that armada gets in the air, we’re done! Down, Wade, to within a few hundred feet of the ground, and close to the field!”

The *Solarite* flashed down in a power dive—down with a sickening lurch. A sudden tremendous weight seemed to crush them as the ship was brought out of the dive not more than two hundred feet from the ground. Close to blacking out, Wade nevertheless shot it in as close to the field as he dared. Anxiously he called to Arcot, who answered with a brief “Okay!” The planes loomed gigantic now, their true proportions showing clearly against the brilliant light of the field. A tremendous wave of sound burst from the loudspeaker as the planes rolled across the ground to leap gracefully into the air—half a million tons of metal!

===Chinese===
*索拉里特号*迅疾爬升，末了悬停在云毯的边缘，灰雾之海里一粒看不见的微尘。脚下，他们看见一片从古丘间开凿出来的巨大场地。这个高度上，比例感全失了。它只像一片寻常的场子，停着十八架寻常的飞机。其中一架正在动，转眼便腾空而起！可整片大场上似乎不见人影——从这个高度，人小得看不见。

阿科特猛地大喝一声：「这就是他们的突然袭击！他们比咱们预想的提前多了！那支大军一旦升空，咱们就完了！降，韦德——降到离地几百英尺，贴到场边去！」

*索拉里特号*动力俯冲而下——一记令人作呕的猛坠。离地不过两百英尺才拉出俯冲，一股可怕的重力仿佛要把他们压扁。韦德眼前发黑，仍把船贴到场边尽可能近的地方。他焦急地喊阿科特，那头简短地回了声「好了！」此刻巨机庞然在前，在场地耀眼的灯光里，真实的比例一览无余。巨机碾过地面、优雅地腾空而起时，扬声器里迸出声浪的巨涛——五十万吨的金属！

===Original===
From the *Solarite* there darted a pale beam of ghostly light, faintly gray, tinged with red and green—the ionized air of the beam. It moved in a swift half circle. In an instant the whirr of the hundreds, thousands of giant propellers was drowned in a terrific roar of air. Great snowflakes fell from the air before them; it was white with the solidified water vapor. Then came a titanic roar and the planet itself seemed to shake! A crash, a snapping and rending as a mighty fountain of soil and rock cascaded skyward, and with it, twisting, turning, hurled in a dozen directions at once, twelve titanic ships reeled drunkenly into the air!

For a barely perceptible interval there was an oppressive silence as the ray was shut off. Then a bedlam of deafening sound burst forth anew, a mighty deluge of unbearable noise as the millions of tons of pulverized rock, humus and metal fell back. Some of it had ascended for miles; it settled amid a howling blizzard—snow that melted as it touched the madly churned airfield.

High above there were ten planes flying about uncertainly. Suddenly one of these turned, heading for the ground far below, its wings screaming their protest as the motors roared, ever faster, with the gravity of the planet aiding them. There was a rending, crackling crash as the wings suddenly bent back along the sides. An instant later the fuselage tore free, rocketing downward; the wings followed more slowly—twisting, turning, dipping in mile-long swoops.

===Chinese===
*索拉里特号*射出一道幽幽的浅光，淡淡的灰，泛着红绿——那是光束电离的空气。它飞快地扫过半个圆。刹那间，成百上千具巨桨的嗡鸣被一声狂暴的气流怒吼吞没。大片雪花从天而落——空气白茫茫一片，全是凝成固体的水汽。接着一声巨响，行星本身仿佛都震了！轰然爆裂、撕扯——一大股泥土与岩石的喷泉冲天而起，随它一起，十二艘巨舰东倒西歪地甩上半空，拧着、转着，霎时朝十几个方向一齐抛出！

光束一收，有那么一瞬，是一片压迫人的死寂。随即震耳的喧嚣再度爆发——几百万吨研碎的岩石、腐殖土和金属砸回地面，一场不堪忍受的声浪洪流。有些飞腾了几英里高，落下来时裹在一片呼啸的暴风雪里——雪一触到疯狂翻搅的机场，便化了。

高处还有十架飞机惶惶乱转。其中一架猛然掉头，直奔脚下的远地，马达咆哮，越冲越快，行星的引力推波助澜，机翼发出抗议的尖叫。啪的一声爆裂——机翼猛然折向机身两侧。转瞬，机身挣脱出来，火箭般直坠；机翼跟得慢些——拧着、转着，一英里一英里地飘荡。

===Original===
The *Solarite* shot away from the spot at maximum speed—away and up, with a force that nailed the occupants to the floor. Before they could turn, behind them flared a mighty gout of light that struck to the very clouds above, and all the landscape, for miles about, was visible in the glare of the released energy.

As they turned, they saw on the plain, below a tremendous crater, in its center a spot that glowed white and bubbled like the top of a huge cauldron.

Nine great planes were circling in the air; then in an instant they were gone, invisible. As swiftly the *Solarite* darted away with a speed that defied the aim of any machine.

High above the planes they went, for with his radar Arcot could trace them. They were circling, searching for the *Solarite*.

The tiny machine was invisible in the darkness, but its invisibility was not revealed by the Kaxorian’s radio detectors. In the momentary lull, Fuller asked a question.

“Wade, how is it that those ships can be invisible when they are driven by light, and have the light stored in them? They’re perfectly transparent. Why can’t we see the light?”

“They are storing the light. It’s bound—it can’t escape. You can’t see light unless it literally hits you in the eye. Their stored light can’t reach you, for it is held by its own attraction and by the special field of the big generators.”

===Chinese===
*索拉里特号*全速蹿离——向上，向外，一股蛮力把乘员钉在地板上。还没等他们转身，身后轰然腾起一大团光柱，直捅云霄，方圆几英里的山川，都在这释出的能量映照下纤毫毕现。

转回来时，他们看见平原上一座巨坑，坑心一点白炽，像一口大锅的锅面咕嘟作响。

九架巨机在空中绕着圈；转瞬便没了——隐形了。*索拉里特号*也随即遁走，快得任何机器都瞄它不住。

他们升到机群上方——凭雷达，阿科特盯得住它们。它们兜着圈，搜寻*索拉里特号*。

小小的飞船隐在黑暗里，卡克索里亚人的无线电探测器却也没能揭穿它。趁这片刻间歇，富勒问了一句。

「韦德，那些船靠光驱动、肚里存着光，怎么还能隐形？它们通体透明。咱们怎么看不见那些光？」

「光被存起来了。它是被缚住的——逃不掉。光非得真撞进你眼睛，你才看得见。他们存的光够不着你，因为它被自身的引力和大发电机的特殊场攥着。」

===Original===
They seemed to be above one of the Kaxorian planes now. Arcot caught the roar of the invisible propellers.

“To the left, Wade—faster—hold it—left—ah!” Arcot pushed a button.

Down from the *Solarite* there dropped a little canister, one of the bombs that Arcot had prepared the night before. To hit an invisible target is ordinarily difficult, but when that target is far larger than the proverbial side of a barn, it is not very difficult, at that. But now Arcot’s companions watched for the crash of the explosion, the flash of light. What sort of bomb was it that Arcot hoped would penetrate that tremendous armor?

Suddenly they saw a great spot of light, a spot that spread with startling rapidity, a patch of light that ran, and moved. It flew through the air at terrific speed. It was a pallid light, green and wan and ghostly, that seemed to flow and ebb.

For an instant Morey and the others stared in utter surprise. Then suddenly Morey burst out laughing.

“Ho—you win, Arcot. That was one they didn’t think of, I’ll bet! Luminous paint—and by the hundred gallon! Radium paint, I suppose, and no man has ever found how to stop the glow of radium. That plane sticks out like a sore thumb!”

===Chinese===
此刻他们似乎正处在一架卡克索里亚巨机的上方。阿科特捕捉到了隐形巨桨的轰鸣。

「左，韦德——快——稳住——左——啊！」阿科特按下按钮。

*索拉里特号*丢下一只小小的罐子——阿科特前夜备下的炸弹之一。要打中隐形的目标，平常是难事；可当那目标比老话说的谷仓门板还大出许多时，倒也不算难。此刻阿科特的同伴们都在等那爆炸的轰响、那闪光。他指望的是什么炸弹，能钻透那骇人的装甲？

蓦地，他们看见一大团光斑，蔓延的神速，一片流动奔走的光。它以骇人的速度掠过空中。那是一团苍白的光，惨绿、幽幽，仿佛在流淌、涨落。

莫雷等人瞠目结舌了一瞬。随即莫雷放声大笑。

「嚯——你赢了，阿科特。这一手他们准没想过！发光漆——一泼就是上百加仑！我猜是镭漆——镭的光，还没人找到法子弄灭。那架飞机现在扎眼得像根肿起的大拇指！」

===Original===
Indeed, the great luminous splotch made the gigantic plane clearly evident against the gray clouds. Visible or not, that plane was marked.

Quickly Arcot tried to maneuver the *Solarite* over another of the great ships, for now the danger was only from those he could not see. Suddenly he had an idea.

“Morey—go back to the power room and change the adjustment on the meteorite avoider to half a mile!” At once Morey understood his plan, and hastened to put it into effect.

The illuminated plane was diving, twisting wildly now. The *Solarite* flashed toward it with sickening speed, then suddenly the gigantic bulk of the plane loomed off to the right of the tiny ship, the great metal hull, visible now, rising in awesome might. They were too near; they shot away to a greater distance—then again that ghostly beam reached out—and for just a fraction of a second it touched the giant plane.

The titanic engine of destruction seemed suddenly to be in the grip of some vastly greater Colossus—a clutching hand that closed! The plane jumped back with an appalling crash, a roar of rending metal. For an instant there came the sound like a mighty buzz-saw as the giant propellers of one wing cut into the body of the careening plane. In that instant, the great power storage tank split open with an impact like the bursting of a world. The *Solarite* was hurled back by an explosion that seemed to rend the very atoms of the air, and all about them was a torrid blaze of heat and light that seemed to sear their faces and hands with its intensity.

===Chinese===
可不是——那大片发光的斑块，把巨机的轮廓在灰云前衬得清清楚楚。隐不隐形，这架飞机算是挂了号。

阿科特随即设法把*索拉里特号*挪到另一艘巨舰上方——此刻危险只来自他看不见的那些。突然，他有了主意。

「莫雷——回动力舱，把避陨器的作用距离调到半英里！」莫雷立时会意，赶紧去办。

那架涂了光的巨机正在俯冲，狂乱地翻拧。*索拉里特号*以叫人头晕的速度扑向它，蓦地，巨机庞大的身躯赫然现于小船右舷，巨大的金属船体森然而起。太近了；他们蹿开些——随后那道幽光又探出去——只一刹那，搭上了巨机。

那台毁灭的巨器仿佛突然落进某个更其浩大的巨灵的掌握——一只收拢的铁钳！巨机向后猛地一跳，一声崩裂的巨响，金属撕裂的咆哮。有一瞬，传来电锯般的尖啸——一侧机翼的巨桨切进了倾侧的机身。就在那一瞬，巨大的蓄力罐豁然迸裂，一声巨响如世界的崩爆。*索拉里特号*被炸力掀了回去——那爆炸仿佛撕碎了空气的原子，四围是一片灼热的光焰，烈得像要燎了他们的脸和手。

===Original===
Then in a time so brief that it seemed never to have happened, it was gone, and only the distant drone of the other ships’ propellers came to them. There was no luminous spot. The radium paint had been destroyed in the only possible way—it was volatilized through all the atmosphere!

The Terrestrians had known what to expect; had known what would happen; and they had not looked at the great ship in that last instant. But the Kaxorians had naturally been looking at it. They had never seen the sun directly, and now they had been looking at a radiance almost as brilliant. They were temporarily blinded; they could only fly a straight course in response to the quick order of their squadron commander.

And in that brief moment that they were unable to watch him, Arcot dropped two more bombs in quick succession. Two bright spots formed in the black night. No longer did these planes feel themselves invulnerable, able to meet any foe! In an instant they had put on every last trace of power, and at their top speed they were racing west, away from their tiny opponent—in the only direction that was open to them.

But it was useless. The *Solarite* could pick up speed in half the time they could, and in an instant Arcot again trained his beam on the mighty splotch of light that was a fleeing plane.

===Chinese===
随后，快得仿佛从未发生，一切都没了，只剩别处巨机桨叶遥远的嗡鸣。发光的斑点没有了。镭漆被唯一可能的法子销毁了——气化，散进了整个大气！

地球人早知道会有什么、会出什么事，最后那一瞬没有去看那巨舰。卡克索里亚人却自然一直望着它。他们从未直视过太阳，而刚才望着的，是一团几乎同样耀眼的光。他们一时全盲了；只能听中队长急促的号令，循直线飞。

趁他们看不了的那一小会儿，阿科特连丢两弹。黑夜里绽出两团亮斑。这些飞机再不觉得自己金刚不坏、无敌不克了！转瞬之间，它们把最后一丝功率全开出来，撒腿向西狂奔，逃离那小小的对手——那是留给它们的唯一出路。

可是没用。*索拉里特号*提速只需它们一半的工夫，转眼，阿科特又把光束对准了那团奔逃的巨影。

===Original===
Out of the darkness came a ghostly beam, for an instant of time so short that before the explosive shells of the other could be trained on it, the *Solarite* had moved. Under that touch the mighty plane began crumbling, then it splintered beneath the driving blow of the great wing, as it shot toward the main body of the plane at several miles a second—driving into and through it! The giant plane twisted and turned as it fell swiftly downward into the darkness—and, again there came that world-rocking explosion, and the mighty column of light.

Again and yet again the *Solarite* found and destroyed Kaxorian super-planes, protected in the uneven conflict by their diminutive size and the speed of their elusive maneuvering.

But to remind the men of the *Solarite* that they were not alone, there came a sudden report just behind them, and they turned to see that one of the energy bombs had barely fallen short! In an instant the comparative midget shot up at top speed, out of danger. It looped and turned, hunting, feeling with its every detector for that other ship. The great planes were spread out now. In every direction they could be located—and all were leaving the scene of the battle. But one by one the *Solarite* shot after them, and always the speed of the little ship was greater.

Two escaped. They turned off their useless invisibility apparatus and vanished into the night.

The *Solarite*, supported by her vertical lift units, coasted toward a stop. The drone of the fleeing super-planes diminished and was gone, and for a time the thrum of the generator and the tap-dance of relays adjusting circuits was the only sound aboard.

===Chinese===
黑暗中探出一道幽光，接触的时间短到对方爆裂弹还来不及瞄准，*索拉里特号*已挪了窝。只这一碰，巨机开始崩解，随后在巨翼以每秒几英里扑向机身的猛击下碎裂——撞进去，穿了过去！巨舰拧着转着，急坠入黑暗——随后，又是那声摇撼世界的爆炸，那根光的巨柱。

一次，又一次，*索拉里特号*寻着、毁着卡克索里亚的超级巨机；在这场力量悬殊的较量里，护着它的，是它的小，和它飘忽机动的快。

可也有人提醒*索拉里特号*上的人：你们不是孤单的——身后突然一声爆响，他们回头，看见一枚能量炸弹险险落在近旁！这相对的小不点顷刻全速蹿起，脱出险境。它兜着圈、转着向，用每一具探测器摸索那条船。巨机此刻散开了，四面八方都寻得着——全都正撤离战场。可*索拉里特号*一艘一艘追上去，小船的速度总是更胜一筹。

两艘逃了。它们关掉无用的隐身机，没入夜色。

*索拉里特号*靠竖直升力机组托着，滑行减速。远遁的超级巨机嗡鸣渐弱、消散，一时间，船上只余发电机的低吟和继电器调路时踢踢踏踏的舞步。

===Original===
Wade sighed finally. “Well, gentlemen, now we’ve got it, what do we do with it?”

“What do you mean?” Morey asked.

“Victory. The Jackpot. Having the devices we just demonstrated, we are now the sole owners, by right of conquest, of one highly disturbed nation of several million people. With that gadget there, we can pick it up and throw it away.

“Personally, I have a feeling that we’ve just won the largest white elephant in history. We don’t just walk off and leave it, you know. We don’t want it. But we’ve got it.

“Our friends in Sonor are not going to want the problem either; they just wanted the Kaxorians combed out of their hair.

“As I say—we’ve got it, now—but what do we do with it?”

“It’s basically their problem, isn’t it?” protested Fuller. Morey looked somewhat stricken, and thoroughly bewildered. “I hadn’t considered that aspect very fully; I’ve been too darned busy trying to stay alive.”

Wade shook his head. “Look, Fuller—it was their problem before, too, wasn’t it? How’d they handle it? If you just let them alone, what do you suppose they’ll do with the problem this time?”

“The same thing they did before,” Arcot groaned. “I’m tired. Let’s get some sleep first, anyway.”

===Chinese===
韦德终于叹了口气：「那么，各位，如今咱们赢了——拿它怎么办？」

「什么怎么办？」莫雷问。

「胜利啊。头彩啊。手里攥着咱们刚演示过的这些家伙，咱们如今凭征服权，独得一个几百万人口、被搅得鸡犬不宁的国度。凭那玩意儿，咱们能把它拎起来扔出去。

「私底下，我觉得咱们刚赢了历史上最大的一头白象。这个，你知道，不能一走了之。咱们不想要它。可它已经在咱们手里了。

「索诺那几位朋友也不会要这个难题；他们只想把卡克索里亚人从头发里梳出去。

「我再说一遍——东西到手了——可咱们拿它怎么办？」

「说到底是他们的问题，不是吗？」富勒抗辩。莫雷显得有些发懵，全然不知所措。「这一层我没细想过；我一直忙着别挨枪子儿都来不及。」

韦德摇头。「听着，富勒——从前这也是他们的问题，对不对？他们怎么处的？要是撒手不管，你猜这回他们又会怎么处？」

「还是老一套。」阿科特呻吟一声，「我累了。不管怎样，先睡一觉再说。」

===Original===
“Sure; that makes good sense,” Wade agreed. “Sleep on it, yes. But go to sleep on it—well, that’s what the not-so-bright Sonorans tried doing.

“And offhand, I’d say we were elected. The Kaxorians undoubtedly have a nice, two thousand year old hatred for the Sonorans who so snobbishly ignored them, isolated them, and considered them unfit for association. The Sonorans, on the other hand, are now thoroughly scared, and will be feeling correspondingly vindictive. They won this time by a fluke—our coming. I can just see those two peoples getting together and settling any kind of sensible, long-term treaty of mutual cooperation!”

Arcot and Morey both nodded wearily. “That is so annoyingly correct,” Morey agreed. “And you know blasted well none of us is going to sleep until we have some line of attack on this white elephant disposal problem. Anybody any ideas?”

Fuller looked at the other three. “You know, in design when two incompatible materials must be structurally united, we tie each to a third material that is compatible with both.

“Sonor didn’t win this fight. Kaxor didn’t win it. Earth—in the *persona* of the *Solarite*—did. Earth isn’t mad at anybody, hasn’t been damaged by anybody, and hasn’t been knowingly ignoring anybody.

“The Sonorans want to be let alone; it won’t work, but they can learn that. I think if we run the United Nations in on this thing, we may be able to get them to accept our white elephant for us.

“They’ll be making the same mistake Sonor did if they don’t—knowingly ignoring the existence of a highly intelligent and competent race. It doesn’t seem to work, judging from history both at home and here.”

===Chinese===
「行，这话说得在理。」韦德附和，「带着问题睡一觉，没错。可是——带着问题『睡死过去』——唉，不那么聪明的索诺人，试的正是这一手。

「而且随口一说，我瞧这差事是落在咱们头上了。卡克索里亚人对索诺人，无疑憋着一股足足两千年陈的恨——恨他们那么势利地无视自己、隔离自己，视自己为不堪交往之辈。索诺人呢，这回吓破了胆，往后自然要挟怨报复。这回他们赢，赢在歪打正着——咱们来了。你只管想想，这两家凑到一处，能谈出什么像样的、长期互助的条约！」

阿科特和莫雷都乏乏地点头。「这话烦人地在理。」莫雷叹道，「而且你也清楚得很，这头白象的处置法子没个眉目，咱们谁也睡不成。谁有主意？」

富勒看看另外三位：「你们知道，设计上要把两种不相容的材料在结构上连起来，咱们就把各自拴到第三种材料上——它跟两边都合得来。

「这一仗不是索诺赢的，也不是卡克索赢的。赢的是地球——以*索拉里特号*为其*化身*。地球没跟谁置气，没受过谁的害，也从不曾故意晾着谁。

「索诺人想独善其身；行不通，可他们学得会。我想，要是咱们把联合国拉进来，说不定能请他们把咱们的白象接过去。

「不这样的话，他们就会重蹈索诺的覆辙——明知有一个高度智慧、高度干练的种族存在，却故意无视。看看家里和这儿的历史，这一手看来行不通。」

===Original===
The four looked at each other, and found agreement.

“That’s something more than a problem to sleep on,” Morey said. “I’ll get in touch with Sonor and tell ’em the shooting is over, so they can get some sleep too.

“It’s obvious a bunch of high-power research teams are going to be needed in both countries. Earth has every reason to respect Sonoran mental sciences as well as Kaxorian light-engineering. And Earth—as we just thoroughly demonstrated—has some science of her own. Obviously, the interaction of the three is to the maximum advantage of each—and will lead to a healing of the breach that now exists.”

Arcot looked up and yawned. “I’m putting this on autopilot at twenty miles up, and going to sleep. We can kick this around for a month anyway—and this is not the night to start.”

“The decision is unanimous,” Wade grinned.

===Chinese===
四人相顾，意见一致。

「这就不止是睡一觉的问题了。」莫雷说，「我去联络索诺，告诉他们枪声停了，好让他们也睡个安稳觉。

「两国显然都得派一批高功率的研究队。地球完全有理由敬重索诺的心智科学，也敬重卡克索的光学工程。而地球——咱们刚才透彻演示过了——也有自己的几手。三者的交融，显然对每一方都是大大的利好——也会把眼下这道裂痕，愈合起来。」

阿科特抬头打了个哈欠。「我把船定在二十英里高空自动巡航，睡觉。这事咱们反正可以磨上一个月——今晚就不开工了。」

「全票通过。」韦德咧嘴一笑。

# The Black Star Passes / 黑星掠过

## Prologue / 序幕

===Original===
Taj Lamor gazed steadily down at the vast dim bulk of the ancient city spread out beneath him. In the feeble light of the stars its mighty masses of up-flung metal buildings loomed strangely, like the shells of some vast race of crustacea, long extinct. Slowly he turned, gazing now out across the great plaza, where rested long rows of slender, yet mighty ships. Thoughtfully he stared at their dim, half-seen shapes.

Taj Lamor was not human. Though he was humanoid, Earth had never seen creatures just like him. His seven foot high figure seemed a bit ungainly by Terrestrial standards, and his strangely white, hairless flesh, suggesting unbaked dough, somehow gave the impression of near-transparency. His eyes were disproportionately large, and the black disc of pupil in the white corneas was intensified by contrast. Yet perhaps his race better deserved the designation *homo sapiens* than Terrestrians do, for it was wise with the accumulated wisdom of uncounted eons.

He turned to the other man in the high, cylindrical, dimly lit tower room overlooking the dark metropolis, a man far older than Taj Lamor, his narrow shoulders bent, and his features grayed with his years. His single short, tight-fitting garment of black plastic marked him as one of the Elders. The voice of Taj Lamor was vibrant with feeling:

“Tordos Gar, at last we are ready to seek a new sun. Life for our race!”

===Chinese===
塔吉·拉莫尔（Taj Lamor）稳稳俯瞰着脚下铺展开的那座古城黑压压的浩大轮廓。星光微弱，幢幢上指的金属巨楼在其中诡谲地耸峙，像某种早已绝灭的巨型甲壳动物的外壳。他缓缓转身，望向大广场——那里停着一列列修长而威武的飞船。他若有所思地凝望它们朦胧半现的身影。

塔吉·拉莫尔不是人类。他虽是人形，地球却从未见过他这样的生灵。他七英尺高的身量，按地球的标准略显笨拙；那身奇怪的白皙无毛的皮肉，像未烤熟的面团，隐隐透着几分近于透明的意思。他的眼睛大得不成比例，白色角膜衬着黑色的瞳仁，对比之下愈显深邃。然而他的种族或许比地球人更配*智人*（*homo sapiens*）这个名号——因为他们胸怀无数亘纪积累下来的智慧。

他转向塔室里另一间俯瞰黑暗都城的另一人——那塔室高踞、浑圆、灯昏。那人比塔吉·拉莫尔苍老得多，窄肩伛偻，岁月把他的面容染成了灰色。他一身黑色塑料的紧身短装，标明他是长老（Elder）中的一员。塔吉·拉莫尔的嗓音饱含感情：

「托尔多斯·加尔（Tordos Gar），我们终于可以动身去寻一颗新的太阳了。为我们种族续命！」

===Original===
A quiet, patient, imperturbable smile appeared on the Elder’s face and the heavy lids closed over his great eyes.

“Yes,” he said sadly, “but at what cost in tranquility! The discord, the unrest, the awakening of unnatural ambitions—a dreadful price to pay for a questionable gain. Too great a price, I think.” His eyes opened, and he raised a thin hand to check the younger man’s protest. “I know—I know—in this we do not see as one. Yet perhaps some day you will learn even as I have that to rest is better than to engage in an endless struggle. Suns and planets die. Why should races seek to escape the inevitable?” Tordos Gar turned slowly away and gazed fixedly into the night sky.

Taj Lamor checked an impatient retort and sighed resignedly. It was this attitude that had made his task so difficult. Decadence. A race on an ages-long decline from vast heights of philosophical and scientific learning. Their last external enemy had been defeated millennia in the past; and through easy forgetfulness and lack of strife, ambition had died. Adventure had become a meaningless word.

===Chinese===
长老脸上浮起一个安静、耐心、波澜不惊的微笑，厚重的眼睑在那双大眼上合了下来。

「是啊，」他悲声道，「可这安宁的代价何其大！纷争、动荡、不该有的野心被唤醒——为一份吉凶未卜的所得，付出的价钱太可怕了。我看，太大了。」他睁开眼，抬起一只瘦手，止住年轻人的争辩。「我知道——我知道——在这件事上咱们看法不一。可是将来有一天，也许你会像我一样明白：歇息，胜过卷入一场无休止的挣扎。太阳和行星都会死。种族又何必逃避这注定的结局？」托尔多斯·加尔缓缓转过身去，凝望夜空。

塔吉·拉莫尔压下一句不耐的反驳，认命地叹了口气。正是这种态度，把他的事业搅得这般艰难。颓败。一个种族，从哲学与科学的巍巍高峰上一路衰颓了亿万年。他们最后一个外敌，早在千百年前就被打垮了；安逸的遗忘和无争无斗，把野心饿死了。冒险，成了一个没有意义的词。

===Original===
Strangely, during the last century a few men had felt the stirrings of long-buried emotion, of ambition, of a craving for adventure. These were throwbacks to those ancestors of the race whose science had built their world. These men, a comparative handful, had been drawn to each other by the unnatural ferment within them; and Taj Lamor had become their leader. They had begun a mighty struggle against the inertia of ages of slow decay, had begun a search for the lost secrets of a hundred-million-year-old science.

Taj Lamor raised his eyes to the horizon. Through the leaping curve of the crystal clear roof of their world glowed a blazing spot of yellow fire. A star—the brightest object in a sky whose sun had lost its light. A point of radiance that held the last hopes of an incredibly ancient race.

The quiet voice of Tordos Gar came through the semidarkness of the room, a pensive, dreamlike quality in its tones.

===Chinese===
奇怪的是，最近这一百年里，有几个人感到了久埋心底的情绪在萌动——野心，和对冒险的渴望。他们是返祖者，回到了那个用科学缔造了这个世界的祖先的模样。这一小撮人，被体内那股反常的躁动彼此吸引到一处；塔吉·拉莫尔成了他们的领袖。他们向亘古缓朽的惰性发起了宏大的抗争，开始寻回一门一亿岁的科学失落的秘密。

塔吉·拉莫尔抬眼望向地平线。透过他们这个世界清澈如晶的穹顶上跃动的弧线，灼灼燃着一点黄色的火。一颗星——太阳熄灭后的天空里最亮的物体。一团光，拴着一个古老得难以想象的种族最后的指望。

托尔多斯·加尔安详的声音穿过半暗的房间，语调里带着一缕沉思的、梦似的意味。

===Original===
“You, Taj Lamor, and those young men who have joined you in this futile expedition do not think deeply enough. Your vision is too narrow. You lack perspective. In your youth you cannot think on a cosmic scale.” He paused as though in thought, and when he continued, it seemed almost as though he were speaking to himself.

“In the far, dim past fifteen planets circled about a small, red sun. They were dead worlds—or rather, worlds that had not yet lived. Perhaps a million years passed before there moved about on three of them the beginnings of life. Then a hundred million years passed, and those first, crawling protoplasmic masses had become animals, and plants, and intermediate growths. And they fought endlessly for survival. Then more millions of years passed, and there appeared a creature which slowly gained ascendancy over the other struggling life forms that fought for the warmth of rays of the hot, red sun.

“That sun had been old, even as the age of a star is counted, before its planets had been born, and many, many millions of years had passed before those planets cooled, and then more eons sped by before life appeared. Now, as life slowly forced its way upward, that sun was nearly burned out. The animals fought, and bathed in the luxury of its rays, for many millennia were required to produce any noticeable change in its life-giving radiations.

“At last one animal gained the ascendancy. Our race. But though one species now ruled, there was no peace. Age followed age while semi-barbaric peoples fought among themselves. But even as they fought, they learned.

===Chinese===
「你，塔吉·拉莫尔，还有那些随你踏上这趟徒劳远征的年轻人，想得不够深。你们的眼界太窄，缺了纵深。你们还年轻，还做不来宇宙尺度的思考。」他顿了顿，似在沉思，再开口时，几乎像自言自语。

「在遥远朦胧的过去，十五颗行星绕着一颗小小的红太阳旋转。它们都是死的世界——或者说，是还没活过来的世界。也许过了一百万年，其中三颗上才有了生命的萌芽。又过了一亿年，那些最初的、蠕动的原生质团块变成了动物、植物，和介于其间的生灵。它们为生存无休止地厮杀。又是几百万年过去，出现了一种生物，慢慢压倒了其余在红星的热焰下争食的生灵。

「那颗太阳，按恒星的年纪算，在行星诞生前就已经老了；又是亿万年过去，行星才冷却；再过不知多少亘纪，生命才现身。如今，生命艰难向上攀的时候，那太阳已近烧空。动物们厮杀着，泡在它光焰的奢华里——它的生养之辐要起一点看得出的变化，得熬上千年万年。

「末了一种动物得了势。我们的种族。可纵然一统了物种，并没有和平。半开化的人群自相攻伐，一个时代接一个时代。可他们一边打，一边学。

===Original===
“They moved from caves into structures of wood and stone—and engineering had its beginning. With the buildings came little chemical engines to destroy them; warfare was developing. Then came the first crude flying-machines, using clumsy, inefficient engines. Chemical engines! Engines so crude that one could watch the flow of their fuel! One part in one hundred thousand million of the energy of their propellents they released to run the engines, and they carried fuel in such vast quantities that they staggered under its load as they left the ground! And warfare became worldwide. After flight came other machines and other ages. Other scientists began to have visions of the realms beyond, and they sought to tap the vast reservoirs of nature’s energies, the energies of matter.

“Other ages saw it done—a few thousand years later there passed out into space a machine that forced its way across the void to another planet! And the races of the three living worlds became as one—but there was no peace.

“Swiftly now, science grew upon itself, building with ever faster steps, like a crystal which, once started, forms with incalculable speed.

“And while that science grew swiftly greater, other changes took place, changes in our universe itself. Ten million years passed before the first of those changes became important. But slowly, steadily our atmosphere was drifting into space. Through ages this gradually became apparent. Our worlds were losing their air and their water. One planet, less favored than another, fought for its life, and space itself was ablaze with the struggles of wars for survival.

===Chinese===
「他们从洞穴搬进木石的房子——工程学发了端。房子一多，又有了毁掉它们的小小化学机器；战争在成形。随后来了第一批粗陋的飞行器，用着笨重低效的发动机。化学发动机！粗陋到看得见燃料在里头流的发动机！推进剂的能量，一千亿分之一百份里只放出一份来驱动机器，而燃料要多到离地时被压得踉踉跄跄！于是战争烧遍了全球。飞行之后，又有别的机器、别的时代。别的科学家开始眺望界外的疆域，去开凿自然能量的深库——物质的能量。

「再几个时代，事成了——几千年后，一台机器冲出太空，强渡虚空，抵达了另一颗行星！三颗活世界的种族合而为一——可仍旧没有和平。

「而今，科学在自身之上疯长，步子越迈越快，像一颗晶体，一旦起了头，便以无从计算的速度成形。

「科学一日千里的时候，别的变化也在发生——我们宇宙自身的变化。过了一千万年，第一批变化才显出分量。可我们的大气在缓缓地、持续地飘散进太空。亘纪流转，这一点渐渐显明。我们的世界在失去空气和水。一颗行星得天独薄，为自己的性命厮打，太空本身被求生的战火映得通明。

===Original===
“Again science helped us. Thousands of years before, men had learned how to change the mass of matter into energy, but now at last the process was reversed, and those ancestors of ours could change energy into matter, any kind of matter they wished. Rock they took, and changed it to energy, then that energy they transmuted to air, to water, to the necessary metals. Their planets took a new lease of life!

“But even this could not continue forever. They must stop that loss of air. The process they had developed for reformation of matter admitted of a new use. Creation! They were now able to make new elements, elements that had never existed in nature! They designed atoms as, long before, their fathers had designed molecules. At last their problem was solved. They made a new form of matter that was clearer than any crystal, and yet stronger and tougher than any metal known. Since it held out none of the sun’s radiations, they could roof their worlds with it and keep their air within!

“This was a task that could not be done in a year, nor a decade, but all time stretched out unending before them. One by one the three planets became tremendous, roofed-in cities. Only their vast powers, their mighty machines made the task possible, but it was done.”

===Chinese===
「又是科学救了咱们。几千年前，人们已会把物质的质量化作能量；如今这过程终于反了过来——我们的祖先能把能量化成物质，要什么物质有什么。他们取来岩石，化成能量，再把那能量炼成空气、水、必需的金属。他们的行星续了一大截命！

「可这也不是长久之计。得把空气的散逸堵住。他们那套物质重铸的工艺，另辟了一条新路——创造！他们造得出崭新的元素，自然界从未有过的元素！他们设计原子，一如更早的先人设计分子。末了，难题解了。他们造出一种新物质，比任何晶体都透亮，却比任何已知的金属都坚韧结实。它一丝太阳的辐射都拦不住——正好拿来给世界做穹顶，把空气关在家里！

「这不是一年、十年做得完的事，可无穷的岁月在他们面前铺展无尽。一颗接一颗，三颗行星变成了盖顶的巨城。凭他们浩大的伟力、威武的机器，这事才成得了——可终究成了。」

===Original===
The droning voice of Tordos Gar ceased. Taj Lamor, who had listened with a mixture of amusement and impatience to the recital of a history he knew as well as the aged, garrulous narrator, waited out of the inborn respect which every man held for the Elders. At length he exclaimed: “I see no point—”

“But you will when I finish—or, at least, I hope you will.” Tordos Gar’s words and tone were gently reproving. He continued quietly:

“Slowly the ages drifted on, each marked by greater and greater triumphs of science. But again and again there were wars. Some there were in which the population of a world was halved, and all space for a billion miles about was a vast cauldron of incandescent energy in which tremendous fleets of space ships swirled and fused like ingredients in some cosmic brew. Forces were loosed on the three planets that sent even their mighty masses reeling drunkenly out of their orbits, and space itself seemed to be torn by the awful play of energies.

“Always peace followed—a futile peace. A few brief centuries or a few millennia, and again war would flame. It would end, and life would continue.

===Chinese===
托尔多斯·加尔絮絮的嗓音停了。塔吉·拉莫尔听着这段自己同那位喋喋不休的老叙述者一样烂熟的历史，一半好笑，一半不耐，只出于每个人对长老与生俱来的敬重，才捱到讲完。末了他终于开口：「我看不出什么名堂——」

「等我讲完你自会看出——至少，我盼着你会。」托尔多斯·加尔的话音和口气是温和的责备。他静静接着说：

「亘纪缓缓漂过，每一个都以更辉煌的科学胜利作注脚。可战争一次接一次。有的战争，一颗行星的人口折了一半；方圆十亿英里的太空成了一口白炽能量的巨鼎，浩大的太空舰队在其中翻搅熔融，如同一剂宇宙配方的料。三颗行星上放出的力，震得它们庞大的躯体醉汉似的跌出轨道，太空本身仿佛都被那能量的肆虐撕开。

「和平总是跟着来——徒劳的和平。短短几个世纪或几千年，战火重燃。战火熄了，生命继续。

===Original===
“But slowly there crept into the struggle a new factor, a darkening cloud, a change that came so gradually that only the records of instruments, made during a period of thousands of years, could show it. Our sun had changed from bright red to a deep, sullen crimson, and ever less and less heat poured from it. It was waning!

“As the fires of life died down, the people of the three worlds joined in a conflict with the common menace, death from the creeping cold of space. There was no need for great haste; a sun dies slowly. Our ancestors laid their plans and carried them out. The fifteen worlds were encased in shells of crystal. Those that had no atmosphere were given one. Mighty heating plants were built—furnaces that burned matter, designed to warm a world! At last a state of stability had been reached, for never could conditions change—it seemed. All external heat and light came from far-off stars, the thousands of millions of suns that would never fail.

“Under stress of the Great Change one scarcely noticed, yet almost incredible, transformation had occurred. We had learned to live with each other. We had learned to think, and enjoy thinking. As a species we had passed from youth into maturity. Advancement did not stop; we went on steadily toward the goal of all knowledge. At first there was an underlying hope that we might some day, somehow, escape from these darkened, artificial worlds of ours, but with the passing centuries this grew very dim and at length was forgotten.

===Chinese===
「可慢慢地，斗争里渗进了一个新因素，一团暗云，一场来得那样徐缓、唯有几千年间的仪器记录才显得出的变化。我们的太阳从亮红转成了深暗的绯红，倾出的热越来越多少。它在衰竭！

「生命的火一黯淡，三个世界的人民便齐心迎战那共同的祸患——太空彻骨的寒冷带来的死亡。倒不必十万火急；一颗太阳死得慢。我们的祖先定下方略，一一办妥。十五颗世界裹进了水晶的壳。没有大气的，配上了大气。建起了浩大的供暖厂——烧物质的炉子，为暖一颗行星而设计的炉子！末了，达到了一种恒态——条件再不会变了，似乎如此。一切外来的光与热都出自遥远的群星，那亿万颗永不会熄灭的太阳。

「在大变的高压之下，一种几乎察觉得不出、却近乎不可思议的转变发生了。我们学会了彼此相处。我们学会了思考，并享受思考。作为一个物种，我们从青年步入了成年。进步没有停；我们朝全部知识的终点稳步前行。起初心底还存着一丝指望，盼着有朝一日、以某种法子，逃出这些幽暗的人造世界；可随着世纪流逝，这点光越来越暗，终于被忘却了。

===Original===
“Gradually as millennia passed, much ancient knowledge was also forgotten. It was not needed. The world was unchanging, there was no strife, and no need of strife. The fifteen worlds were warm, and pleasant, and safe. Without fully realizing it, we had entered a period of rest. And so the ages passed; and there were museums and libraries and laboratories; and the machines of our ancestors did all necessary work. So it was—until less than a generation ago. Our long lives were pleasant, and death, when it came, was a sleep. And then—”

“And then,” Taj Lamor interrupted, a sharp edge of impatience in his tone, “some of us awakened from our stupor!”

The Elder sighed resignedly. “You cannot see—you cannot see. You would start that struggle all over again!” His voice continued in what Taj Lamor thought of as a senile drone, but the younger man paid scant attention. His eyes and thoughts were centered on that brilliant yellow star, the brightest object in the heavens. It was that star, noticeably brighter within a few centuries, that had awakened a few men from their mental slumbers.

===Chinese===
「千年万年过去，许多古老的学问也被忘却了。用不着了。世界恒定不变，没有争斗，也无需争斗。十五颗世界温暖、适意、安稳。不知不觉间，我们进了一个歇息的年代。时代便这样过去；有博物馆，有图书馆，有实验室；先人的机器包揽了一切的活。如此这般——直到不满一代人之前。我们漫长的生命安逸舒适，死亡来时，也无非一场睡眠。可后来——」

「后来，」塔吉·拉莫尔插进来，语气锋利得不耐，「我们中间有些人从沉睡里醒过来了！」

长老认命地叹道：「你看不见——你看不见。你们要把那场挣扎从头再来一遍！」他的声音接着往下淌——塔吉·拉莫尔只当是老糊涂的絮叨——年轻人几乎没听。他的眼睛和心思都拴在那颗明亮的黄星上，全天穹最亮的物体。正是那颗星——几百年里肉眼可见地亮了起来——把几个人从心智的酣睡中唤醒了。

===Original===
They were throwbacks, men who had the divine gift of curiosity; and sparked by their will to know, they had gone to the museums and looked carefully at the ancient directions for the use of the telectroscope, the mighty electrically amplified vision machine, had gazed through it. They had seen a great sun that seemed to fill all the field of the apparatus with blazing fire. A sun to envy! Further observation had revealed that there circled about the sun a series of planets, five, definitely; two more, probably; and possibly two others.

Taj Lamor had been with that group, a young man then, scarcely more than forty, but they had found him a leader and they had followed him as he set about his investigation of the ancient books on astronomy.

How many, many hours had he studied those ancient works! How many times had he despaired of ever learning their truths, and gone out to the roof of the museum to stand in silent thought looking out across the awful void to the steady flame of the yellow star! Then quietly he had returned to his self-set task.

===Chinese===
他们是返祖者，生来怀着好奇这份天赐之礼；求知欲一点着，他们便钻进博物馆，仔细研读远望镜（telectoscope）——那台电力放大的视觉巨机——的古用法说明，凑上去观望。他们看见一颗大太阳，烈焰熊熊，仿佛灌满了仪器的整个视场。一颗叫人眼热的太阳！进一步的观察显示，绕着这颗太阳转着一系列行星：五颗，确凿无疑；两颗，多半是；另有两颗，也未可知。

塔吉·拉莫尔就在那伙人中间——那时还年轻，四十出头些——可他们认定他是领袖，跟着他一头扎进了古代天文典籍的考究。

他在那些古书里泡了多少个时辰啊！多少回心灰意冷，以为学不出个所以然，走到博物馆的顶上，默然伫立，隔着可怖的虚空，望那黄星安定的焰！然后又悄悄回到自定给自己的功课里。

===Original===
With him as teacher, others had learned, and before he was seventy there were many men who had become true scientists, astronomers. There was much of the ancient knowledge that these men could not understand, for the science of a million centuries is not to be learned in a few brief decades, but they mastered a vast amount of the forgotten lore.

They knew now that the young, live sun, out there in space, was speeding toward them, their combined velocities equalling more than 100 miles each second. And they knew that there were not seven, but nine planets circling about that sun. There were other facts they discovered; they found that the new sun was far larger than theirs had ever been; indeed, it was a sun well above average in size and brilliance. There were planets, a hot sun—a home! Could they get there?

When their ancestors had tried to solve the problem of escape they had concentrated their work on the problem of going at speeds greater than that of light. This should be an impossibility, but the fact that the ancients had tried it, seemed proof enough to their descendants that it was possible, at least in theory. In the distant past they had needed speeds exceeding that of light, for they must travel light years; but now this sun was coming toward them, and already was less than two hundred and fifty billion miles away!

===Chinese===
他以师带徒，旁人也学了起来；到他七十岁前，已有许多人成了真正的科学家、天文学家。古学问里有的是这些人啃不动的东西——百万个世纪的学问，短短几十年学不完——可那被遗忘的渊薮，他们掌握了大半。

如今他们知道了：那颗年轻的、活着的太阳正在太空里朝他们扑来，两下合起来的速度超过每秒 100 英里。他们还知道，绕那颗太阳转的不是七颗，而是九颗行星。别的发现还有：新太阳远比他们那颗旧日的庞大；实在说，论块头与光焰，都远在平均之上。有行星，有烈日——一个家！他们到得了吗？

当年祖先们求解逃亡之题时，把功夫全押在了超光速上。这本该是不可能的，可古人既然试过，在后裔看来便是够硬的凭据——至少理论上可行。远古他们需要超光速，因为要跨越光年；可如今这颗太阳正朝他们来，已经不到两千五百亿英里了！

===Original===
They would pass that other star in about seventy years. That was scarcely more than a third of a man’s lifetime. They could make the journey with conceivable speeds—but in that brief period they must prepare to move!

The swift agitation for action had met with terrific resistance. They were satisfied; why move?

But, while some men had devoted their time to arousing the people to help, others had begun doing work that had not been done for a long, long time. The laboratories were reopened, and workshops began humming again. They were making things that were new once more, not merely copying old designs.

Their search had been divided into sections, search for weapons with which to defend themselves in case they were attacked, and search for the basic principles underlying the operation of their space ships. They had machines which they could imitate, but they did not understand them. Success had been theirs on these quests. The third section had been less successful. They had also been searching for secrets of the apparatus their forefathers had used to swing the planets in their orbits, to move worlds about at will. They had wanted to be able to take not only their space ships, but their planets as well, when they went to settle on these other worlds and in this other solar system.

===Chinese===
他们约莫七十年后将与那颗恒星交会。这不过是一个人寿命的三分之一。以想得到的光速，这趟旅程走得成——可就在这短短一程里，他们必须把迁移备齐！

求速动的那阵疾呼，撞上了天大的阻力。他们心满意足；何必挪窝？

但有人致力于唤醒民众相帮，也有人干起了很久很久没人间津的活。实验室重开了，工场重新嗡嗡作响。他们又造起了新东西——不再是照抄旧图纸。

他们的探索分作几摊：找武器，以防遭袭时自卫；找他们飞船运转背后的基本原理。有现成的机器可供仿制，他们却不解其中之理。这几摊都成了。第三摊不那么顺。他们还在寻一桩秘密——先人用来拨动行星轨道、随意挪移世界的装置的秘密。他们巴望着远赴那个太阳系、落脚那些世界时，带上的不只是飞船，还有行星。

===Original===
But the search for this secret had remained unrewarded. The secret of the spaceships they learned readily, and Taj Lamor had designed these mighty ships below there with that knowledge. Their search for weapons had been satisfied; they had found one weapon, one of the deadliest that their ancestors had ever invented. But the one secret in which they were most interested, the mighty force barrage that could swing a world in its flight through space, was lost. They could not find it.

They knew the principles of the driving apparatus of their ships, and it would seem but a matter of enlargement to drive a planet as a ship, but they knew this was impossible; the terrific forces needed would easily be produced by their apparatus, but there was no way to apply them to a world. If applied in any spot, the planet would be torn asunder by the incalculable strain. They must apply the force equally to the entire planet. Their problem was one of application of power. The rotation of the planet made it impossible to use a series of driving apparatus, even could these be anchored, but again the sheer immensity of the task made it impossible.

===Chinese===
偏偏这桩秘密，寻而无所获。星际飞船的秘密他们学得快，下面广场上那些巨舰，就是塔吉·拉莫尔凭那份知识设计的。武器的搜寻也有了着落——他们找到一件，祖先发明过的最致命的之一。唯独他们最上心的那桩秘密——能拨得动一颗世界在太空里飞驰的伟力阵——失传了。寻不着。

飞船推进装置的原理他们懂，把行星当船推，似乎只消放大便是；可他们知道这行不通：需要的骇人之力，他们的装置出得绰绰有余，却没有法子施到一颗行星上。任凭一点受力，行星都会被无从计算的应力撕碎。必须把力均均地布满整颗行星。他们的难题在功率的施法。行星自转，一串推进装置没法用——即便锚得住；再说，这工程浩大到根本没有可能。

===Original===
Taj Lamor gazed down again at the great ships in the plaza below. Their mighty bulks seemed to dwarf even the huge buildings about them. Yet these ships were his—for he had learned their secrets and designed them, and now he was to command them as they flew out across space in that flight to the distant star.

He turned briefly to the Elder, Tordos Gar. “Soon we leave,” he said, a faint edge of triumph in his voice. “We will prove that our way is right.”

The old man shook his head. “You will learn—” he began, but Taj Lamor did not want to hear.

He turned, passed through a doorway, and stepped into a little torpedo-shaped car that rested on the metal roof behind him. A moment later the little ship rose, and then slanted smoothly down over the edge of the roof, straight for the largest of the ships below. This was the flagship. Nearly a hundred feet greater was its diameter, and its mile and a quarter length of gleaming metal hull gave it nearly three hundred feet greater length than that of the ships of the line.

===Chinese===
塔吉·拉莫尔又俯瞰广场上的巨舰。它们庞大的身躯，连四周的巨厦都衬小了。可这些船是他的——秘密是他参透的，图纸是他画的；如今它们即将飞越太空、远征那颗恒星，统帅也是他。

他短暂地转向长老托尔多斯·加尔。「不久我们就动身，」他说，嗓音里透着一丝得胜的锋芒，「我们会证明，我们的路是对的。」

老人摇头。「你会明白的——」他刚起头，塔吉·拉莫尔却不想听。

他转身穿过一道门，跨进停在他身后金属顶面上的一艘鱼雷形小车。片刻后小船升起，随即顺着屋顶边缘平滑地斜掠而下，直奔下面最大的一艘船。那是旗舰。它的直径比别的船大出近百英尺，一英里又四分之一长的亮闪闪金属船身，比战列舰又长出近三百英尺。

===Original===
This expedition was an expedition of exploration. They were prepared to meet any conditions on those other worlds—no atmosphere, no water, no heat, or even an atmosphere of poisonous gases they could rectify, for their transmutation apparatus would permit them to change those gases, or modify them; they knew well how to supply heat, but they knew too, that that sun would warm some of its planets sufficiently for their purposes.

Taj Lamor sent his little machine darting through the great airlock in the side of the gigantic interstellar ship and lowered it gently to the floor. A man stepped forward, opened the door for the leader, saluting him briskly as he stepped out; then the car was run swiftly aside, to be placed with thousands of others like it. Each of these cars was to be used by a separate investigator when they reached those other worlds, and there were men aboard who would use them.

Taj Lamor made his way to a door in the side of a great metal tube that threaded the length of the huge ship. Opening the door he sat down in another little car that shot swiftly forward as the double door shut softly, with a low hiss of escaping air. For moments the car sped through the tube, then gently it slowed and came to rest opposite another door. Again came the hissing of gas as the twin doors opened, and Taj Lamor stepped out, now well up in the nose of the cruiser. As he stepped out of the car the outer and inner doors closed, and, ready now for other calls, the car remained at this station. On a ship so long, some means of communication faster than walking was essential. This little pneumatic railway was the solution.

===Chinese===
这趟远征是探路之行。那些世界上的任何境况他们都备了应对——没有大气、没有水、没有热，甚至有一层他们可以改造的有毒大气，因为他们的嬗变装置容许他们改换或调理那些气体；供热之道他们谙熟于心。他们也知道，那颗太阳会把它的几颗行星晒得足够暖。

塔吉·拉莫尔驾着小机器，箭一般穿过星际巨舰侧面的大气闸，轻轻落到舱底。一个人上前替领袖开车门，利落地敬礼，看他步出；小车随即被飞快推到一边，同成千上万辆一样的车摆在一处。抵达那些世界后，这些车每辆归一名考察员使用，船上用得上它们的人有的是。

塔吉·拉莫尔走向一根贯穿巨舰全长的大金属管侧壁的门。开门坐进另一辆小车，双层门轻轻合拢，泄气的嘶声一低，小车便箭也似地前蹿。它在管中疾驰片刻，缓缓减速，停在另一扇门对面。双门开启，又是气体的一嘶，塔吉·拉莫尔跨出来，已到了这艘巨舰高高的船首。他下车时内外门相继闭合，小车停在此站，候着下一趟差。船身这么长，比步行快的交通是必需的。这条小小的气动铁道便是答案。

===Original===
As Taj Lamor stepped out of the tube, a half-dozen men, who had been talking among themselves, snapped quickly to attention. Following the plans of the long-gone armies of their ancestors, the men of the expedition had been trained to strict discipline; and Taj Lamor was their technical leader and the nominal Commander-in-Chief, although another man, Kornal Sorul, was their actual commander.

Taj Lamor proceeded at once to the Staff Cabin in the very nose of the great ship. Just above him there was another room, walled on all sides by that clear, glass-like material, the control cabin. Here the pilot sat, directing the motions of the mighty ship of space.

Taj Lamor pushed a small button on his desk and in a moment a gray disc before him glowed dimly, then flashed into life and full, natural color. As though looking through a glass porthole, Taj Lamor saw the interior of the Communications Room. The Communications Officer was gazing at a similar disc in which Taj Lamor’s features appeared.

===Chinese===
塔吉·拉莫尔一出管道，六个原本正交谈的人啪地立正。远征队员们按先人久已消逝的军制操练，纪律严明；塔吉·拉莫尔是他们的技术领袖、名义上的总司令——实际的司令是另一个人，科纳尔·索鲁尔（Kornal Sorul）。

塔吉·拉莫尔径直走进巨舰最前端的参谋舱。头顶上还有一舱，四壁都是那种玻璃似的透明材料——操纵舱。驾驶员坐在那里，指挥这台太空巨舰的一举一动。

塔吉·拉莫尔按了下桌上的小钮，面前的一只灰色圆盘先是幽幽发光，随即亮起，映出全彩。像隔着玻璃舷窗似的，他看见了通讯室的内部。通讯官正望着一只同样的圆盘，盘中显出塔吉·拉莫尔的面容。

===Original===
“Have they reported from Ohmur, Lorsand, and Throlus, yet, Morlus Tal?” asked the commander.

“They are reporting now, Taj Lamor, and we will be ready within two and one half minutes. The plans are as before; we are to proceed directly toward the Yellow Star, meeting at Point 71?”

“The plans are as before. Start when ready.”

The disc faded, the colors died, and it was gray again. Taj Lamor pulled another small lever on the panel before him, and the disc changed, glowed, and was steady; and now he saw the preparations for departure, as from an eye on the top of the great ship. Men streamed swiftly in ordered columns all about and into the huge vessels. In an incredibly short time they were in, and the great doors closed behind them. Suddenly there came a low, dull hum through the disc, and the sound mounted quickly, till all the world seemed humming to that dull note. The warning!

===Chinese===
「奥穆尔（Ohmur）、洛尔桑德（Lorsand）和思罗卢斯（Throlus）那边报告了吗，莫鲁斯·塔尔（Morlus Tal）？」司令问。

「正在报告，塔吉·拉莫尔，两分半钟后我们就绪。计划照旧：直奔黄星，71 号点会合？」

「计划照旧。就绪即发。」

圆盘里的颜色褪尽，重新灰了。塔吉·拉莫尔又扳了下面前面板上的小杆，圆盘一变、一亮、稳住了；这一回他看到的是启航的准备，视角来自巨舰顶上的一只眼睛。人流成列，迅疾地绕着巨舰、涌进那些庞然大物。难以置信的短促之后，人已登船，巨门在身后合拢。突然，圆盘里传来一阵低哑的嗡鸣，声音急速攀升，直到整个世界仿佛都嗡着那个沉音。警报！

===Original===
Abruptly the city around him seemed to blaze in a riot of colored light! The mighty towering bulks of the huge metal buildings were polished and bright, and now, as the millions of lights, every color of the spectrum, flashed over all the city from small machines in the air, on the ground, in windows, their great metal walls glistening with a riot of flowing color. Then there was a trembling through all the frame of the mighty ship. In a moment it was gone, and the titanic mass of glistening metal rose smoothly, quickly to the great roof of their world above them. On an even keel it climbed straight up, then suddenly it leaped forward like some great bird of prey sighting its victim. The ground beneath sped swiftly away, and behind it there came a long line of ships, quickly finding their position in the formation. They were heading toward the giant airlock that would let them out into space. There was but one lock large enough to permit so huge a ship to pass out, and they must circle half their world to reach it.

On three other worlds there were other giant ships racing thus to meet beyond their solar system. There were fifty ships coming from each planet; two hundred mighty ships in all made up this armada of space, two hundred gargantuan interstellar cruisers.

One by one the giant ships passed through the airlock and out into space. Here they quickly reformed as they moved off together, each ship falling into its place in the mighty cone formation, with the flagship of Taj Lamor at the head. On they rushed through space, their speed ever mounting. Suddenly there seemed to leap out of nowhere another mass of shining machines that flew swiftly beside them. Like some strange, shining ghosts, these ships seemed to materialize instantly beside and behind their fleet. They fell in quickly in their allotted position behind the flagship’s squadron. One—two more fleets appeared thus suddenly in the dark, and together the ships were flashing on through space to their goal of glowing fire ahead!

===Chinese===
蓦地，四周的城像炸开了一场彩光的狂欢！高耸的金属巨楼打磨得锃亮，此刻千万盏灯——光谱上的每一色——自空中、地面、窗口的小机器里泼洒全城，巨楼的金属墙面流光溢彩，斑斓涌动。随后，一声轻颤传遍巨舰周身。转瞬即逝，那庞然的闪光巨物平稳而迅速地升起，升向头顶世界的巨大穹顶。它端平了船身垂直上爬，随即猛地前扑，像一头瞥见猎物的巨禽。大地急速退去，身后拉出一条长长的舰列，迅速各就编队位。他们正朝那座放他们出太空的巨大气闸飞去。够大的气闸只有一座，他们得绕行半个世界才到得了。

另外三颗世界上，另有巨舰如此竞发，要到太阳系外汇合。每颗行星出五十艘；两百艘巨舰凑成这支太空大军——两百艘庞大的星际巡洋舰。

巨舰一艘接一艘穿过气闸，进入太空。它们随即在共同进发中重整队形，各舰落进巨大的锥形阵里的位置，塔吉·拉莫尔的旗舰居首。它们在太空中疾驰，速度节节攀升。蓦地，仿佛凭空跳出又一批闪亮的机器，飞快地伴在侧翼。像一群古怪的、发亮的幽灵，这些舰只霎时在他们舰队的侧后现了形，迅速编入旗舰中队身后的既定位置。一队——又是两队——就这样在黑暗中骤现；众舰一道，朝前方那团烧灼的目标掠过太空！

===Original===
Hour after hour, day after day the ships flashed on through the awful void, the utter silence relieved by the communications between themselves and the slowly weakening communications from the far-off home planets.

But as those signals from home grew steadily weaker, the sun before them grew steadily larger. At last the men began to feel the heat of those rays, to realize the energy that that mighty sea of flame poured forth into space, and steadily they watched it grow nearer.

Then came a day when they could make out clearly the dim bulk of a planet before them, and for long hours they slowed down the flying speed of the ships. They had mapped the system they were approaching; there were nine planets of varying sizes, some on the near and some on the far side of the sun. There were but three on the near side; one that seemed the outermost of the planets, about 35,000 miles in diameter, was directly in their path, while there were two more much nearer the sun, about 100,000,000 and 70,000,000 miles distant from it, each about seven to eight thousand miles in diameter, but they were on opposite sides of the sun. These more inviting and more accessible worlds were numbers two and three of the planetary system. It was decided to split the expedition into two parts; one part was to go to planet two, and the other to three. Taj Lamor was to lead his group of a hundred ships to the nearer planet at once.

===Chinese===
一小时又一小时，一天又一天，舰队掠过可怖的虚空；唯有舰与舰之间的通讯，和远方母星渐弱的讯息，纾解着这份彻底的寂静。

可母星的信号越来越弱时，前方的太阳却越来越大。末了，人们开始感到那些光焰的热，认识到那片火的汪洋泼进太空的能量，眼看着它稳步逼近。

终于有一天，他们能清楚辨出前方一颗行星朦胧的轮廓，便长时间减了舰速。他们将临的那个星系早已绘过图：九颗行星大小不一，有的在太阳这一侧，有的在那一侧。近侧只有三颗；一颗像是行星中最靠外的，直径约 35,000 英里，正挡在航线上；另两颗离太阳近得多，约各距 100,000,000 英里和 70,000,000 英里，直径各约七八千英里，却分处太阳两侧。这两颗更迷人、也更可及的世界，是行星系的二号和三号。于是议定：远征军一分为二，一部赴二号行星，一部赴三号。塔吉·拉莫尔即刻率他那一百艘赴较近的行星。

===Original===
In a very brief time the great ships slanted down over what seemed to be a mighty globe of water. They were well in the northern hemisphere, and they had come near the planet first over a vast stretch of rolling ocean. The men had looked in wonder at such vast quantities of the fluid. To them it was a precious liquid, that must be made artificially, and was to be conserved, yet here they saw such vast quantities of natural water as seemed impossible. Still, their ancient books had told of such things, and of other strange things, things that must have been wondrously beautiful, though they were so old now, these records, that they were regarded largely as myths.

Yet here were the strange proofs! They saw great masses of fleecy water vapor, huge billowy things that seemed solid, but were blown lightly in the wind. And natural air! The atmosphere extended for hundreds of miles off into space; and now, as they came closer to the surface of this world the air was dense, and the sky above them was a beautiful blue, not black, even where there were stars. The great sun, so brilliantly incandescent when seen from space, and now a glowing globe of reddish-yellow.

And as they came near land, they looked in wonder at mighty masses of rock and soil that threw their shaggy heads high above the surrounding terrain, huge masses that rose high, like waves in the water, till they towered in solemn grandeur miles into the air! What a sight for these men of a world so old that age long erosion had washed away the last traces of hills, and filled in all of the valleys!

===Chinese===
不多时，巨舰们斜斜压向一颗望去看全是水的行星。他们在北半球，最先掠过的是一大片起伏的洋。众人惊叹地望着那么大量的液体。在他们，水是珍贵的物事，得人工造、须节省着用；可这儿，天然的水多得不像话。何况古书上讲过这些，还讲过别的奇观——想必曾美得惊人，只是那些记载太老了，多半被当作神话。

而奇观的实证就在眼前！他们看见大团大团絮状的水汽，蓬蓬硕硕仿佛实体，却被风轻轻吹动。还有天然的空气！大气一直铺出去几百英里；此刻贴近这世界的表面，空气稠了，头顶的天是一片好看的蓝——不是黑的，连缀着星星的地方也是。那颗大太阳，从太空里看时白炽刺目，如今成了一团红黄的光球。

临近陆地时，他们惊叹地望着大团大团的岩石与土壤，把毛茸茸的头颅高高抛出四野之上，隆起如水中的浪，直至庄严巍峨地插进几英里的高空！这对这些来自一个老得连岁月的剥蚀都洗尽了丘陵、填平了谷地的世界的人，是何等景象！

===Original===
In awe they looked down at the mighty rock masses, as they swung low over the mountains, gazing in wonder at the green masses of the strange vegetation; strange, indeed, for they for uncounted ages had grown only mushroom-like cellulose products, and these mainly for ornament, for all their food was artificially made in huge factories.

Then they came over a little mountain lake, a body of water scarcely large enough to berth one of their huge ships, but high in the clear air of the mountains, fed by the melting of eternal snows. It was a magnificent sapphire in a setting green as emerald, a sparkling lake of clear water, deep as the sea, high in a cleft in the mountains.

In wonder the men looked down at these strange sights. What a marvelous home!

Steadily the great machines proceeded, and at last the end of the giant mountain was reached, and they came to a great plain. But that plain was strangely marked off with squares, as regularly as though plotted with a draftsman’s square. This world must be inhabited by intelligent beings!

===Chinese===
他们敬畏地俯瞰岩体，低低掠过群山，惊奇地望着一片片绿色的陌生植被；实在陌生——他们不知多少亘纪以来只种得出蘑菇似的纤维产物，且多半为观瞻——口粮一律出自大工厂的人工合成。

随后他们来到一座小小的山中湖上——一片水，勉强泊得下他们一艘巨舰，却高悬在山间清冽的空气里，靠万古积雪的消融滋养。它是一粒华贵的蓝宝石，嵌在翡翠般的绿里，一泓清亮的湖水，深可拟海，高卧山坳。

众人惊叹地俯瞰这些陌生的景致。何等美妙的家！

巨舰稳步前行，末了到了大山尽头，来到一片大平原。可那平原划着一格格的方块，规整得像用制图员的角尺打出来的。这世界必有智慧的生民！

===Original===
Suddenly Taj Lamor saw strange specks off in the far horizon to the south, specks that seemed to grow in size with terrific velocity; these must be ships, the ships of these people, coming to defend their home. The strangely pallid face of Taj Lamor tightened into lines of grim resolution. This was a moment he had foreseen and had dreaded. Was he to withdraw and leave these people unmolested, or was he to stand and fight for this world, this wonderfully beautiful home, a home that his race could live in for millions of years to come? He had debated this question many times before in his mind, and he had decided. There would never, never be another chance for his people to gain a new home. They must fight.

Swiftly he gave his orders. If resistance came, if an attack were made, they were to fight back at once, with every weapon at their disposal.

The strangers’ ships had grown swiftly larger to the eye, but still, though near now, they seemed too small to be dangerous. These giant interstellar cruisers were certainly invulnerable to ships so small; their mere size would give them protection! These ships were scarcely as long as the diameter of the smaller of the interstellar ships—a bare two hundred and fifty feet for the largest.

===Chinese===
蓦地，塔吉·拉莫尔望见南方远处地平线上一串陌生的斑点，正以骇人的速度变大；那必是船——这些人的船，来保卫他们的家了。塔吉·拉莫尔苍白的脸绷紧成一线冷峻的决意。这一刻他预见过，也惧怕过。是退走、不去搅扰这些人，还是立足、为这个世界而战——这个美妙绝伦的家，一个他的种族可以住上几百万年的家？这问题他在心里掂过千百回，他已决断。他的人民再不会有、再不会有第二个得到新家的机会。必须一战。

他飞快下了令：若有抵抗、若遭攻击，即刻还手，手里的兵器一概用上。

陌生人的船在视野里迅速变大，可就算近了，它们仍小得构不成威胁。这些星际巨舰对这么小的船，当然刀枪不入——光是块头就是护身符！这些船，最长的也不过同星际船中较小者的直径相当——顶天二百五十英尺。

===Original===
The interstellar cruisers halted in their course, and waited for the little ships to approach. They were fast, for they drew alongside quickly, and raced to the front of the flagship. There was one small one that was painted white, and on it there was a large white banner, flapping in the wind of its passage. The rest of the ships drew off as this came forward, and stopped, hanging motionless before the control room of the giant machine. There were men inside—three strange men, short and oddly pink-skinned—but they were gesturing now, motioning that the giant machine settle to the ground beneath. Taj Lamor was considering whether or not to thus parley with the strangers, when suddenly there leaped from the white craft a beam of clear white—a beam that was directed toward the ground, then swung up toward the great cruiser in a swift arc!

As one, a dozen swift beams of pale red flared out from the giant and bathed the pygmy craft. As they reached it, the white ray that had been sweeping up suddenly vanished, and for an instant the ship hung poised in the air; then it began to swing crazily, like the pendulum of a clock—swung completely over—and with a sickening lurch sped swiftly for the plain nearly five miles below. In moments there came a brief flare, then there remained only a little crater in the soft soil.

But the red beams had not stopped with the little ship; they had darted out to the other machines, trying to reach them before they could bring those strange white rays into play. The cruisers obviously must win, for they carried dozens of projectors, but they might be damaged, their flight delayed. They must defeat those strangers quickly. The rays of Taj Lamor’s ship lashed out swiftly, but almost before they had started, all the other ships, a full hundred, were in action, and the flagship was darting swiftly up and away from the battle. Below, those pale red rays were taking a swift toll of the little ships, and nearly twenty of them rolled suddenly over, and dashed to destruction far below.

===Chinese===
星际巡洋舰停了航，等小船来近。它们真快，转眼贴到近旁，抢到了旗舰前头。有一艘小白船，挂着一面白底大旗，在航风中猎猎作响。其余的船闪开，让它上前，悬停在巨舰操纵室前一动不动。舱里有人——三个奇怪的人，矮小，皮肤是奇异的粉红——正在打手势，示意巨舰降落到下面的地上去。塔吉·拉莫尔正掂量要不要如此同陌生人谈判，那白船突然射出一道净白的光——先指向地面，随即一道急弧，扫向巨舰！

刹那间，十二条淡红的光束从巨舰身上迸出，罩住那艘侏儒小船。光束一及，那条上扫的白光骤然消失；小船在半空悬了一瞬，随即疯癫地摆起来，像钟摆——荡了个满圆——带着一阵叫人头晕的猛坠，直扑下面近五英里的平原。片刻后火光一闪，松软的泥土里只剩一个小小的弹坑。

可红光没有停在小船上；它们直扑别的机器，想赶在那些奇怪的白光出手之前够着它们。巡洋舰显然必胜——它们载着几十台投射器——可它们也可能受伤，航程受阻。必须速战速决。塔吉·拉莫尔的座舰光束疾挥，可几乎同时，其余百艘全部投入了战斗，旗舰则疾速上蹿，脱离战团。下方，淡红的光束飞快收割着小船，近二十艘猛然翻倒，栽向深处毁灭。

===Original===
But now the little ships were in swift darting motion. Because of their small size, they were able to avoid the rays of the larger interstellar cruisers, and as their torpedo-shaped hulls flashed about with bewildering speed, they began to fight back. They had been taken utterly by surprise, but now they went into action with an abandon and swiftness that took the initiative away from the gigantic interstellar liners. They were in a dozen places at once, dodging and twisting, unharmed, out of the way of the deadly red beams, and were as hard to hit as so many dancing feathers suspended over an air jet.

And if the pilots were skillful in avoiding enemy rays, their ray men were as accurate in placing theirs. But then, with a target of such vast size, not so much skill was necessary.

These smaller vessels were the ships of Earth. The people of the dark star had entered the solar system quite unannounced, except that they had been seen in passing the orbit of Mars, for a ship had been out there in space, moving steadily out toward Neptune, and the great interstellar cruisers, flashing in across space, away from that frigid planet, had not seen the tiny wanderer. But he had seen those mighty hulks, and had sent his message of danger out on the ether, warning the men of Earth. They had relayed it to Venus, and the ships that had gone there had received an equally warm reception, and were even now finding their time fully occupied trying to beat off the Interplanetary Patrol.

The battle ended as swiftly as it began, for Taj Lamor, in his machine high above, saw that they were outclassed, and ordered them to withdraw at once. Scarcely ten minutes had elapsed, yet they had lost twenty-two of their giant ships.

The expedition that had gone to Venus reported a similarly active greeting. It was decided at once that they should proceed cautiously to the other planets, to determine which were inhabited and which were not, and to determine the chemical and physical conditions on each.

The ships formed again out in space, on the other side of the sun, however, and started at once in compact formation for Mercury.

Their observations were completed without further mishap, and they set out for their distant home, their number depleted by forty-one ships, for nineteen had fallen on Venus.

===Chinese===
可小船们动起来了，疾如飞镖。凭着小巧，它们躲得开星际巨舰的光束；鱼雷形的船身以叫人眼花缭乱的速度四下翻飞，开始还手。它们被杀了个措手不及，此刻却以一股不管不顾的迅猛夺回了主动，把这些庞然的星际班轮甩在后头。它们同时出现在十几个地方，闪转腾挪，无伤地滑出致命红光的路径，难打得很——像气流上悬着的一蓬蓬舞动的羽毛。

驾驶员躲敌光躲得巧，光炮手打敌舰打得准。不过，靶子那么大，本也用不着多少准头。

这些小船，是地球的船。黑暗之星的子民闯进太阳系，事先无人知晓——只在过火星轨道时被撞见了一回：太空里有艘船正稳稳向海王星飞去，星际巨舰自那颗冻行星掠入太空时，没瞧见那个小小的游子。可它瞧见了那些庞然大物，把危险的讯息送上了以太，警告地球人。讯息转发到了金星；开到那里的舰队也领受了同样热辣的接待，此刻正忙得脚不沾地，招架行星际巡逻队的围攻。

战斗结束得同开始一样快——高坐上方机器里的塔吉·拉莫尔看出了劣势，即刻下令撤退。前后不过十分钟，他们已折了二十二艘巨舰。

远赴金星的那支也回报了一场同样热闹的迎接。当即议定：谨慎探查其余行星，查明哪些有生民、哪些没有，并查明各自的天文与地学境况。

舰队在太阳另一侧的太空里重新集结，随即以密集队形直取水星。

他们的勘察一路无事地做完，便踏上归程，回他们遥远的家园去了——减员四十一艘：金星折了十九艘。

## I / 一

===Original===
The Terrestrian and Venerian governments had met in conference, a grim, businesslike discussion with few wasted words. Obviously, this was to be a war of science, a war on a scale never before known on either world. Agreements were immediately drawn up between the two worlds for a concerted, cooperative effort. A fleet of new and vastly more powerful ships must be constructed—but first they must have a complete report on the huge invading craft that had fallen in western Canada, and on Venus, for they might conceivably make their secrets their own.

They called for the scientists whose work had made possible their successful resistance of the marauders: Arcot, Morey and Wade. They found them working in the Arcot Laboratories.

“Wade,” called Arcot tensely as he snapped the switch of the televisophone, “bring Morey and meet me at the machine on the roof at once. That was a call from Washington. I’ll explain as soon as you get there.”

On the roof Arcot opened the hangar doors, and entered the five-passenger molecular motion ship inside. Its sleek, streamlined sides spoke of power and speed. This was a special research model, designed for their experiments, and carrying mechanisms not found in commercial crafts. Among these were automatic controls still in the laboratory stage, but permitting higher speed, for no human being could control the ship as accurately as these.

It took the trio a little less than a quarter of an hour to make the 5,000 mile trip from New York to the battlefield of Canada. As they sped through the air, Arcot told them what had transpired. The three were passed through the lines at once, and they settled to the ground beside one of the huge ships that lay half buried in the ground. The force of the impact had splashed the solid soil as a stone will splash soft mud, and around the ship there was a massive ridge of earth. Arcot looked at the titanic proportions of this ship from space, and turned to his friends:

===Chinese===
地球和金星两国政府开了个会——一场冷峻的、公事公办的商讨，没有一句废话。显然，这将是一场科学的战争，规模为两个世界前所未见。两个世界当即立约，通力协作。必须造出一支功率远为强大的新舰队——但首先，得把加拿大西部和金星坠毁的那些入侵巨舰摸个底朝天，说不定能把它们的秘密变成自己的。

他们召来了那几位凭一己之力顶住掳掠者的科学家：阿科特、莫雷和韦德。三人在阿科特实验室里被寻着了。

「韦德，」阿科特啪地打开电视电话，急促地喊，「带上莫雷，马上到楼顶机器这儿同我会合。华盛顿来的电话。你们一到我就说。」

在楼顶，阿科特拉开机棚门，走进那艘五座的分子运动飞船。它光洁的流线侧影透着功率与速度。这是艘特制的研究型号，为他们的实验而设计，装着商用机上看不到的机构。其中有几套还在实验室阶段的自动控制，却容许更高的速度——因为再没有哪个人能把船操纵得像它们这样精确。

从纽约到加拿大战场 5,000 英里的路程，三人用了不到一刻钟。一路疾飞，阿科特把原委讲了。他们随即被放行穿过防线，降落在一艘半埋进土里的巨舰旁。撞击之力把坚实的泥土溅得如石头溅开烂泥，巨舰四周拱起一道厚重的土脊。阿科特打量着这艘太空巨舰堂皇的比例，转向朋友们：

===Original===
“We can investigate that wreck on foot, but I think it’ll be far more sensible to see what we can do with the car. This monster is certainly a mile or more long, and we’d spend more time in walking than in investigation. I suggest, we see if there isn’t room for the car inside. This beats even those huge Kaxorian planes for size.” Arcot paused, then grinned. “I sure would have liked to mix in the fight they must have had here—nice little things to play with, aren’t they?”

“It would make a nice toy,” agreed Wade as he looked at the rows of wicked-looking projectors along the sides of the metal hull, “and I wonder if there might not be some of the crew alive in there? If there are, the size of the ship would prevent their showing themselves very quickly, and since they can’t move the ship, it seems to me that they’ll let us know shortly that they’re around. Probably, with the engines stopped, their main weapons are useless, but they would doubtless have some sort of guns. I’m highly in favor of using the car. We carry a molecular director ray, so if the way is blocked, we can make a new one.”

Wade’s attention was caught by a sudden flare of light a few miles across the plain. “Look over there—that ship is still flaming—reddish, but almost colorless. Looks like a gas flame, with a bit of calcium in it. Almost as if the air in the ship were combustible. If we should do any exploring in this baby, I suggest we use altitude suits—they can’t do any harm in any case.”

Three or four of the great wrecks, spread over a wide area, were burning now, hurling forth long tongues of colorless, intensely hot flame. Several of the ships had been only slightly damaged; one had been brought down by a beam that had torn free the entire tail of the ship, leaving the bow in good condition. Apparently this machine had not fallen far; perhaps the pilot had retained partial control of the ship, his power failing when he was only a comparatively short distance from Earth. This was rather well to one side of the plain, however, and they decided to investigate it later.

===Chinese===
「这残骸咱们可以走着查，可我看，用飞船行事要明智得多。这怪物少说也有一英里长，走路的时间准保比查的时间长。我提议，看看里面有没有地方容咱们的船落脚。论块头，连卡克索里亚那些巨机都比它不过。」阿科特顿了顿，咧嘴一笑。「他们这儿准打过一仗，真想掺和掺和——玩意儿不错吧？」

「当玩具倒是件好玩具，」韦德望着金属船身侧一排排凶相毕露的投射器，附和道，「就是不知道里面还有没有活着的乘员？如果有，船这么大，他们一时半会儿露不了面；船又动不了，我看他们很快就会想法子让咱们知道他们在。多半引擎一停，主武器就废了，可他们八成还揣着什么枪。我举双手赞成用飞船。咱们带着分子定向射线呢，路要是堵了，就现开一条。」

平原上几英里外一处突发的火光攫住了韦德的注意。「瞧那边——那条船还在烧——发红，可又几乎无色。像气火，掺了点钙似的。简直像船里的空气是可燃的。要进这宝贝探个究竟，我提议穿高空服——横竖没坏处。」

三四艘巨骸散布在一片广大的区域里烧着，吐出一条条无色灼热的长火舌。有几艘只受了轻伤；一艘被一道光束撕掉了整条船尾，船首完好。看来这架机器没摔多远；也许驾驶员到离地球不远处才断了力，还保住了对船的部分控制。只是它偏在平原很远的一侧，他们决定回头再查。

===Original===
The ship nearest them had crashed nose first, the point being crushed and shattered. Arcot maneuvered his craft cautiously toward the great hole at the nose of the ship, and they entered the mighty vessel slowly, a powerful spotlight illuminating the interior. Tremendous girders, twisted and broken by the force of impact, thrust up about them. It soon became evident that there was little to fear from any living enemies, and they proceeded more rapidly. Certainly no creature could live after the shock that had broken these huge girders! Several times metal beams blocked their path, and they were forced to use the molecular director ray to bend them out of the way.

“Man,” said Arcot as they stopped a moment to clear away a huge member that was bent across their path, “but those beams do look as if they were built permanently! I’d hate to ram into one of them! Look at that one—if that has anywhere near the strength of steel, just think of the force it took to bend it!”

At last they had penetrated to the long tube that led through the length of the ship, the communication tube. This admitted the small ship easily, and they moved swiftly along till they came to what they believed to be about the center of the invader. Here Arcot proposed that they step out and see what there was to be seen.

===Chinese===
离他们最近的一艘是头朝下栽的，船头压得粉碎。阿科特小心地把飞船朝船头的大洞里挪，缓缓驶进这庞然大物，一盏强力的探照灯照亮内部。巨梁被撞击之力拧断，七扭八歪地戳在四周。很快便看得出，活着的敌人不足为惧，他们加快了速度。震断这些巨梁的那一击之后，断没有生灵还能活命！几次金属横梁挡路，都用分子定向射线扳开了。

「老天，」他们停下来清一根横在路上的大梁时，阿科特说，「这些梁造得跟打算用一万年似的！撞上一根我可爱不了！瞧那根——要是强度赶得上钢，想想弯折它得多大的力！」

末了，他们进到了那根贯穿全船的长管——交通管。小船进出自如，他们一路疾行，到了自以为是入侵者船腹中心的地方。阿科特提议下船，看看有什么可看的。

===Original===
The others agreed, and they at once put on their altitude suits of heavy rubberized canvas, designed to be worn outside the ship when at high altitude, or even in space. They were supplied with oxygen tanks that would keep the wearer alive for about six hours. Unless the atmosphere remaining in the alien ship was excessively corrosive, they would be safe. After a brief discussion, they decided that all would go, for if they met opposition, there would be strength in numbers.

They met their first difficulty in opening the door leading out of the communication tube. It was an automatic door, and resisted their every effort—until finally they were forced to tear it out with a ray. It was impossible to move it in any other way. The door was in what was now the floor, since the ship seemed to have landed on one side rather than on its keel.

They let themselves through the narrow opening one at a time, and landed on the sloping wall of the corridor beyond.

“Lucky this wasn’t a big room, or we’d have had a nice drop to the far wall!” commented Wade. The suits were equipped with a thin vibrating diaphragm that made speech easy, but Wade’s voice came through with a queerly metallic ring.

Arcot agreed somewhat absently, his attention directed toward their surroundings. His hand light pierced the blackness, finally halting at a gaping opening, apparently the entrance to a corridor. As they examined it, they saw that it slanted steeply downward.

===Chinese===
众人赞成，随即套上厚帆布涂胶的高空服——那是为高空乃至太空的舱外作业设计的。氧气罐够维持约六小时。只要外星船里残存的大气不是腐蚀性过强，他们就安全。略一合计，决定全员同去——万一遇敌，人多势众。

头一道难题出在交通管的出舱门上。那是扇自动门，怎么弄都不肯就范——末了只好用射线把它撕了下来。别的法子一概使不动它。如今船是侧卧而非坐底，那门正在「地板」上。

他们一个个从窄洞里溜下去，落在走廊倾斜的壁上。

「幸亏这屋不大，不然咱们得来一趟漂亮的跳墙！」韦德说。服装上装着一层薄薄的振动膜片，说话不难，可韦德的声音透出来，带着一股古怪的金属腔。

阿科特心不在焉地应着，注意力全在四周。他的手电刺破黑暗，最后停在一个豁口上——看去是条走廊的入口。细看之下，走廊陡峭地斜着向下。

===Original===
“It seems to be quite a drop,” said Wade as he turned his light into it, “but the surface seems to be rather rough. I think we can do it. I notice that you brought a rope, Morey; I think it’ll help. I’ll go first, unless someone else wants the honor.”

“You go first?” Arcot hesitated briefly. “But I don’t know—if we’re all going, I guess you had better, at that. It would take two ordinary men to lower a big bulk like you. On the other hand, if anybody is going to stay, you’re delegated as elevator boy!

“Hold everything,” continued Arcot. “I have an idea. I think none of us will need to hold the weight of the others with the rope. Wade, will you get three fairly good-sized pieces of metal, something we can tie a rope to? I think we can get down here without the help of anyone else. Morey, will you cut the rope in three equal pieces while I help Wade tear loose that girder?”

Arcot refused to reveal his idea till his preparations were complete, but worked quickly and efficiently. With the aid of Wade, he soon had three short members, and taking the rope that Morey had prepared, he tied lengths of cord to the pieces of metal, leaving twenty foot lengths hanging from each. Now he carefully tested his handiwork to make sure the knots would not slip.

===Chinese===
「这一下可深得很，」韦德把手电照进去，「不过壁面挺糙，我看得下去。我看见你带绳子了，莫雷；有用。我先下，除非哪位想要这份荣幸。」

「你先下？」阿科特略一犹豫。「可我说不好——既然都去，那还是你去吧。放下你这副大块头，得两个寻常人。反过来，谁要留下，你就被点名当电梯员了！

「都别忙，」阿科特接着说，「我有个主意。咱们谁都不用靠绳子吊谁的重量。韦德，去捡三块够大的金属，能拴绳子的那种。我看不用别人帮，咱们自己下得去。莫雷，把绳子裁成三等份，我和韦德去拆那根梁。」

阿科特备齐之前死活不肯亮出主意，手脚却麻利。有韦德搭手，三根短梁很快到手；他拿莫雷备好的绳，往金属块上各拴一股，每头垂下二十英尺。他细细检验了活计，保险扣不会滑。

===Original===
“Now, let’s see what we can do.” Arcot put a small loop in one end of a cord, thrust his left wrist through this, and grasped the rope firmly with his hand.
